People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I keep coming up with reasons to stay in the back, even when I’m supposed to be up front for work.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding that area because you're afraid of having a panic attack or some other uncomfortable feeling there?\n\nPatient: Not really a panic attack, no. It’s more that I just can’t make myself go near those front windows or that part of the street anymore. I keep finding excuses to stay in the back, even though I know I’m supposed to be up front for work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the front windows or that part of the street that you're afraid of or trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: It’s that spot itself, I guess—the front windows and the street right outside. I just feel like I can’t go near it now. I keep making excuses to stay in the back, even though I know being up front is part of my job.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think might happen to you if you were to go near the front windows or that part of the street?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can name one exact thing I think will happen. I just know I can’t make myself go near those front windows or that stretch of street anymore. When I’m at work, I keep coming up with reasons to stay in the back, even though I’m supposed to be up front too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about that area by the front windows or the street that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: It’s just that area itself, yeah. The front windows and the street right outside them. I can’t make myself go over there anymore, so I keep finding reasons to stay in the back, even though I know that’s part of my job.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about that area that scares you or makes you want to avoid it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s that front area itself—the windows and the street right outside. I don’t really have a better way to explain it, I just can’t make myself go near it now. I keep staying in the back and making excuses, even though I know I’m supposed to work up front too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something outside those front windows that you're afraid of seeing?\n\nPatient: It’s not really one specific thing I’m afraid of seeing. It’s more like I can’t make myself go near those windows or that part of the street at all. I just keep staying in the back and making excuses, even though I’m supposed to be up there too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you fear might happen or something specific you fear seeing when you're near the front windows or that part of the street?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s one specific thing I’m afraid I’ll see, or one exact thing I think will happen. It’s just… being near those front windows and that part of the street makes me freeze up, and I can’t make myself go over there. I keep making excuses to stay in the back, even though I know I’m supposed to be working up front too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in that area that you used to be able to tolerate but now can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Before, I could work up front by the windows or go by that part of the street without thinking about it. Now I just can’t make myself do it. I keep staying in the back and coming up with excuses, even though I know it’s part of my job.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about that area that you're afraid of?\n\nPatient: It’s not really one specific thing, I guess. It’s that area itself now—the front windows, that stretch of the street. I just can’t make myself go near it anymore, and I keep finding excuses to stay in the back even though I’m supposed to be up there for work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s more that I just can’t make myself go near those front windows or that part of the street anymore. I keep finding excuses to stay in the back, even though I know I’m supposed to be up front for work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the front windows or that part of the street that you're afraid of or trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: It’s that spot itself, I guess—the front windows and the street right outside. I just feel like I can’t go near it now. I keep making excuses to stay in the back, even though I know being up front is part of my job.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you think might happen to you if you were to go near the front windows or that part of the street?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can name one exact thing I think will happen. I just know I can’t make myself go near those front windows or that stretch of street anymore. When I’m at work, I keep coming up with reasons to stay in the back, even though I’m supposed to be up front too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about that area by the front windows or the street that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: It’s just that area itself, yeah. The front windows and the street right outside them. I can’t make myself go over there anymore, so I keep finding reasons to stay in the back, even though I know that’s part of my job.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about that area that scares you or makes you want to avoid it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s that front area itself—the windows and the street right outside. I don’t really have a better way to explain it, I just can’t make myself go near it now. I keep staying in the back and making excuses, even though I know I’m supposed to work up front too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something outside those front windows that you're afraid of seeing?\n\nPatient: It’s not really one specific thing I’m afraid of seeing. It’s more like I can’t make myself go near those windows or that part of the street at all. I just keep staying in the back and making excuses, even though I’m supposed to be up there too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you fear might happen or something specific you fear seeing when you're near the front windows or that part of the street?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s one specific thing I’m afraid I’ll see, or one exact thing I think will happen. It’s just… being near those front windows and that part of the street makes me freeze up, and I can’t make myself go over there. I keep making excuses to stay in the back, even though I know I’m supposed to be working up front too.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in that area that you used to be able to tolerate but now can't?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Before, I could work up front by the windows or go by that part of the street without thinking about it. Now I just can’t make myself do it. I keep staying in the back and coming up with excuses, even though I know it’s part of my job.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about that area that you're afraid of?\n\nPatient: It’s not really one specific thing, I guess. It’s that area itself now—the front windows, that stretch of the street. I just can’t make myself go near it anymore, and I keep finding excuses to stay in the back even though I’m supposed to be up there for work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something that you think will happen to you if you go to that area that is causing you to avoid it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say exactly what I think will happen. I keep coming up with reasons to stay in the back, even when I’m supposed to be up front for work.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding that area because you're afraid of having a panic attack or some other uncomfortable feeling there?\n\nPatient: Not really a panic attack, no. It’s more that I just can’t make myself go near those front windows or that part of the street anymore. I can’t make myself go over there anymore, so I keep finding reasons to stay in the back, even though I know that’s part of my job.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about that area that scares you or makes you want to avoid it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s that front area itself—the windows and the street right outside.
